2.       At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee and informed 
the members that the Secretary, Department of Health and Family Welfare, and other 
officials have been invited in connection with the examination of the Demands for Gra nts 
(2022-23) of the Department of Health & Family Welfare. *** 
3.       The Chairman then welcomed the Secretary and other officials of the Department and 
enquired whether the allocation of Rs.   83,000 crore in BE 2022 -23 under Demand No. 46 
was sufficien t to achieve the desired objective of the Department of Health and Family 
Welfare. The Chairman also directed the Secretary to focus on the projected demand, actual 
allocation, shortage of funds for any scheme and further requirement of the Department in 
terms of budgetary allocations under this Demand for fiscal year 2022-23.  
4.           Thereafter, the Committee heard the presentation of Secretary Department of Health 
and Family Welfare. The Secretary in his presentation made the following submission be fore 
the Committee:- 
i. The focus is on adopting a holistic approach to Health and well being; 
ii. The promotive , preventive and curative aspects of wellbeing is based on the four 
pillars of Ayushman Bharat which are AB HWC, AB PMJAY, AB DHM and PM 
ABHIM; 
iii. The Health outlay of Rs. 83, 000 crore in BE 2022 -23 has seen a 16.46% increase 
with respect to Rs. 71. 268.77 crore allocation to Health in BE 2021-22; 
iv. The allocation to NHM is Rs. 37, 000 crore as compared to Rs. 34,447 crore in RE 
2021-22; 
v. The allocation to P MSSY is Rs. 10,000 crore as compared to Rs. 7, 400 crore in RE 
2021-22; 
vi. The total budgetary funds allocated to the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare is 
Rs. 83,000 crore against the projection of Rs. 93,299 crore in BE 2022-23 leading to a 
shortfall of Rs. 10,299.75 crore; 
vii. The expenditure in the Health sector has been more than 90% in the past few years; 
viii. Benefit to cost ratio for key healthcare intervention is 10:1; 
ix. As per the 2017 Global Monitoring Report, WHO and World Bank, "Average 4.16% 
of the population annually slips into poverty due to catastrophic health expenditure; 
x. As per the target of achieving government‘s health expenditure to 2.5% of GDP by 
2025, Central and State Governments‘ budgeted expenditure on health sector reached 
2.1 percent of GDP in 2021-22, against 1.3 percent in 2019-20; 
xi.  Proactive approach & regular follow up aided in managing spread of infection; 
xii. There has been a consistent decline in Case Positivity across country; 
xiii. Rs.20,308.70 Crore was approved as "Emergency Covid Response Package (ECRP-
II) for Health Systems Preparedness; 
___________________________________________________________________________ 
*** relates to other matter 

90 
 
xiv. Total isolation beds (with/without oxygen) increased to more than 23.2 lakhs as 
compared to 10,180 before lockdown; 
xv. The number of ICU beds has increased to more than 1.45 lakh as compared to just 
2,168 before lockdown; 
xvi. Comprehensive Primary Healthcare Program (CPHC - NCD) application developed 
for population-based screening; 
xvii. Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission (earlier known as National Digital Health Mission) 
aims to create a seamless online platform enabling interoperability within the digital 
health ecosystem to create a longitudinal Electronic Health Record (EHR) for citizens 
duly following privacy and security norms; 
xviii. eSanjeevani Telemedicine services (doctor to doctor and patient to doctor) is 
operational in 36 States/UTs; 
xix. Under ‗Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Package (PMGKP) Insurance Scheme, 22.12 
lakhs frontline heath workers including ASHAs fighting COVID-19 have been 
insured; 
xx. As on 17 th February 2022, more than 173.87 crore total doses of Covid -19 vaccine 
have been administered; 
xxi. Pools under NHM (NRHM & NUHM) have been merged from F.Y. 2022-23. 
5.          Thereafter, Additional Secretary & Mission Di rector, NHM elaborated on the 
different components of the National Health Mission. He submitted that the Pools under 
NHM have been merged from F.Y. 2022 -23 to provide flexibility to States/UTs, improve 
administrative efficiency, minimize the human interfac e involved in multiple instances of 
funds withdrawal and improve financial utilization of the States/UTs. He further submitted 
that the Department of Expenditure approved NHM Budget of FY 2022 -23 in three pools: (i) 
Flexible Pool for RCH & Health System St rengthening, National Health Programme and 
Urban Health Mission; (ii) Infrastructure Maintenance(Reimbursement of salaries of regular 
ANMs, LHVs, MPWs etc.) and (iii) Strengthening of National Programme Management Unit 
under NRHM. 
6.         The Secretary,  thereafter, apprised the Committee about the PM -Ayushman Bharat 
Health Infrastructure Mission (PM -ABHIM) which was launched on 25th October -2021 and 
Rs. 64,180 crores was allocated for the period 2021 -22 to 2025 -26. The Secretary further 
submitted that 1. 5 lakh Health and Wellness Centres are set to be established under 
Ayushman Bharat-Health & Wellness Centres by 2022. Hypertension, Diabetes, Oral Cancer, 
breast Cancer, Cervical Cancer Screenings are being provided through HWCs and 
Yoga/Wellness Sessions are also being conducted. More than 170 Free Essential Medicines 
and more than 60 Free Diagnostic Services can be accessed at these centres. Over 2.54 crore 
tele-consultations have been conducted across the country through the eSanjeevani app. For 
strengthening the Community Process, 10.78 lakh Accredited Social Health Activists 
(ASHA) (as on 30 th September, 2021) have been engaged;  5.55 lakh Village Health 
Sanitation and Nutrition Committees (VHSNCs) and 33,800 Rogi Kalyan Samitis (Patient 
Welfare Societi es) have been constituted; VHSNC and RKS have been empowered with 
Untied Grants and their decision making powers have been enhanced.  

91 
 
7.  Elaborating on India's performance on Health indicators, the Secretary submitted that the 
Maternal mortality ratio (MM R) declined by 80% and the Under 5 Mortality Rate (U5MR) 
declined by 71% in India. India has achieved replacement level of fertility and 31 States/UT 
have a Total Fertility Rate (TFR) less than 2. Intensified Mission Indradhanush (IMI -4.0) is 
scheduled pha se wise during Feb 2022 to May 2022 and ―Make in India‖ Pneumococcal 
Conjugate Vaccine (PCV) has been expanded nationwide under Universal Immunization 
Programme. The Secretary further submitted that 18 States/UTs have declared to end TB by 
2025 and TB Inci dence in India has reduced from 217/lakh population in 2015 to 188/lakh 
population in 2020. He also apprised the Committee on the medical education reforms over 
the last 7 years and submitted that on the lines of National Medical Commission and Allied 
and Healthcare Professions Act, 2021, reforms in Nursing and Dental Education is underway.  
8.         The Secretary then apprised the Committee on the objectives of PM -ABHIM, 
different components under Centrally Sponsored Schemes and Central Sector Schemes an d 
the progress made under AB -PMJAY. He further submitted the expenditure trend under 
PMSSY and apprised the Committee on the functional status of 22 AIIMS Projects. 
9.          During the course of the meeting, Members raised the following queries:- 
i. The meager increase in health budget 2022-23 with respect to allocation made in RE 
2022-23; 
ii. The expenditure cuts made in last year; 
iii. High Out of pocket expenditure in Health; 
iv. Need for increasing ICU beds and upgrading infrastructure in the country 
particularly the rural areas; 
v. Status of Clinical trials and efficacy of the new vaccine Corbyvax; 
vi. Provisions regarding diagnostic Centres in rural areas; 
vii. Regional imbalances in the availability of tertiary healthcare services; 
viii. Status of Healthcare infrastructure in Bihar; 
ix. Need for a National Digital Health Record; 
x. Functional status of AIIMS Darbhanga; 
xi. Preparatory Steps for impending pandemic wave; 
xii. Special Budget for Covid vaccination and its expenditure profile ; 
xiii. The timeline for complete 100% vaccination of the population';  
xiv. Need for more improvement in Institutional Delivery Data; 
xv. Doubts on NEXT Exam; 
xvi. Monitoring mechanism and timely inspection of medical colleges; 
xvii. Status of functioning of AIIMS Kalyani; 
xviii. Special efforts needed for TB eradication; 
xix. Upgradation of District Medical Colleges; 
xx. Low remuneration to ASHA workers; 
xxi. Grants under Rogi Kalyan Samiti; and 
xxii. Inclusion of more beneficiaries under AB etc. 
 
10.       The Secretary, Ministry of Health and Family Welfare  responded to the query 
regarding the shortfall in Budget of the Ministry and informed the Committee that the 
consolidated projection of the Ministry was for Rs.93,000 crores and there is a shortfall of 
around Rs.10,000 crores. The need for extra allocatio n was due to the creation of Wellness 
Centres under Ayushman Bharat and the intervention under PM -ABHIM where a large 

92 
 
infrastructure, at the block level and at the district level, is proposed in a saturation mode. 
There was also requirement of funds under Human Resource for Health (HRH) and Two 
Schemes under FSSAI namely, "Indigenous Food Streets" and "Strengthening of Food 
Testing System in the Country Including Provision of Mobile Food Testing Labs". He further 
submitted that Sputnik -V or Zycov -D or Corbe vax, are under Emergency Use Authorisation 
and the public roll -out of Corbevax has not begun. The Committee was also informed that 
Ayushman Bharat PMJAY's coverage is hampered by the fact that it is based on socio -
economic and castes census of 2011. The SE CC data is already outdated and the National 
Health Authority is actively exploring various permutations, combinations and options to 
increase the beneficiary base of Ayushman Bharat PMJAY. On the Status of AIIMS Madurai, 
the Secretary submitted that the cost of Madurai AIIMS compared to other AIIMS which is in 
the region of Rs. 1200 -1400 crores became Rs. 2,000 crores.   So, the Ministry of Health and 
Family Welfare had to take the further approval of Rs. 2,000 crores.   The process to establish 
a project ma nagement unit will be established in the next one month followed by actual 
tendering and allocation of work would start. The Secretary further submitted that AIIMS 
New Delhi has prepared a redevelopment Plan and after its approval the implementation 
work will soon begin. 
11.       The Chairman directed the Secretary to furnish detailed written replies to the queries 
raised by the Members that remained unanswered.   
12.       A verbatim record of the proceedings of the meeting was kept. 
13.       The Committ ee then adjourned at 5:00 p.m to meet again at 11:00 am on 22 nd 
February, 2022
